```
Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the project. Let me compile a detailed report:

## ReleaseBeacon Project Exploration Report

### 1. Overall Project Structure

**ReleaseBeacon** (internal module: `github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard`) is an event-driven release management system with a Go backend, PostgreSQL database, and Next.js frontend. The project uses a single-binary deployment model where the Go server embeds the Next.js static export.

**Key directories:**
- `/cmd/server` - Main Go entry point
- `/internal` - Core backend packages (6,418 lines of Go code across 60 files)
  - `api/` - REST API handlers and middleware (25 handler files)
  - `ingestion/` - Polling workers and webhook handlers
  - `pipeline/` - Configurable processing pipeline
  - `db/` - Database connection and migrations
  - `models/` - Domain models (ReleaseEvent IR)
  - `queue/` - River job queue setup
  - `routing/` - Notification routing (planned)
  - `agents/` - LLM orchestration (planned)
- `/web` - Next.js 15 frontend with TypeScript + Tailwind CSS
- `/docs` - Architecture and design documentation
  - `/designs/` - API and component specifications
  - `/plans/` - Implementation plans with detailed task breakdowns

---

### 2. Implementation Status: What's Built vs Planned

**IMPLEMENTED & WORKING:**

1. **Ingestion Layer (Complete)**
   - `IIngestionSource` interface - abstract polling provider
   - Docker Hub poller implementation (`dockerhub.go`)
   - GitHub webhook handler (`github.go`)
   - Source loader with ID caching (`loader.go`)
   - Orchestrator for coordinated polling (`orchestrator.go`)
   - Service layer normalizing to ReleaseEvent IR (`service.go`)
   - PostgreSQL store with transactional outbox pattern (`pgstore.go`)

2. **Pipeline (Core Nodes Implemented)**
   - Node interface with sequential execution (`node.go`)
   - Regex Normalizer node - parses versions, applies filters
   - Subscription Router node - checks project subscriptions
   - Urgency Scorer node - composite urgency calculation
   - Pipeline runner with job tracking (`runner.go`)
   - River queue integration (`worker.go`)
   - Pipeline job table with `current_node`, `node_results`, progress tracking

3. **REST API (Complete)**
   - All endpoint handlers implemented:
     - Projects CRUD (5 endpoints)
     - Releases read-only (4 endpoints)
     - Subscriptions CRUD (5 endpoints)
     - Sources CRUD + release lookup (7 endpoints)
     - Notification Channels CRUD (5 endpoints)
     - Providers metadata (1 endpoint)
     - Health checks and stats (2 endpoints)
     - SSE real-time events (1 endpoint)
     - GitHub webhooks (1 endpoint)
   - Middleware stack: request ID, logging, recovery, rate limiting, auth (bearer token), CORS
   - PostgreSQL store implementing all interfaces (`pgstore.go`)
   - SSE broadcaster backed by PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY (`events.go`)

4. **Frontend (Complete - Mocked)**
   - Next.js 15 App Router with static export
   - Full page hierarchy implemented:
     - Dashboard with stats cards and activity feed
     - Projects list/detail/create/edit
     - Releases list with pipeline visualization
     - Sources list/create/edit
     - Subscriptions list/create/edit
     - Channels list/create/edit
   - MSW (Mock Service Worker) intercepting all `/api/v1/*` calls
   - Realistic mock data matching API spec
   - SWR for client-side data fetching
   - shadcn/ui component library integrated
   - Tailwind CSS v4 styling
   - Sidebar navigation, header, responsive layout

5. **Database**
   - Full schema with projects, sources, releases, subscriptions, channels, api_keys, pipeline_jobs
   - Transactional outbox pattern for zero-loss ingestion
   - PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY for real-time events

6. **Testing**
   - Comprehensive test files across all layers
   - Unit tests for pipeline nodes, API handlers, ingestion sources
   - Mocked dependencies for testability

**PLANNED (Not Yet Implemented):**

1. **Routing Layer** (`internal/routing/`) - Notification matrix
2. **Agents Layer** (`internal/agents/`) - LLM orchestration and SRE validation
3. **Advanced Pipeline Nodes:**
   - Availability Checker
   - Risk Assessor
   - Adoption Tracker
   - Changelog Summarizer (LLM-based)
   - Validation Trigger (SRE agent)
4. **Real API Backend** - Currently frontend uses MSW mocks; real backend needs to be called
5. **Plugin System** - Design supports external plugin nodes via gRPC or HTTP callbacks

---

### 3. Web Directory Status

**Current State:** Fully functional dashboard with mocked API

**Directory Structure:**
```
web/
├── app/                  # Next.js 15 App Router pages
│   ├── page.tsx         # Dashboard
│   ├── projects/        # Project CRUD pages (4 pages)
│   ├── releases/        # Release list & detail (2 pages)
│   ├── sources/         # Source CRUD pages (4 pages)
│   ├── subscriptions/   # Subscription CRUD pages (4 pages)
│   ├── channels/        # Channel CRUD pages (4 pages)
│   └── layout.tsx       # Root layout with sidebar
├── components/
│   ├── ui/              # shadcn/ui components (16 components)
│   ├── layout/          # Sidebar & header
│   ├── dashboard/       # Stats cards, recent releases, activity feed
│   ├── projects/        # Project form, pipeline config editor
│   ├── releases/        # Release table, pipeline status visualization
│   ├── sources/         # Source form
│   ├── subscriptions/   # Subscription form
│   └── channels/        # Channel form
├── lib/
│   ├── api/
│   │   ├── types.ts     # TypeScript types matching API spec
│   │   └── client.ts    # Typed fetch wrappers
│   ├── mock/

│   │   ├── handlers.ts  # MSW request handlers (264 lines)
│   │   ├── sse.ts       # Mock SSE emitter for live updates
│   │   └── browser.ts   # MSW browser worker setup
│   ├── msw-provider.tsx # React component to initialize MSW
│   └── utils.ts         # Helper functions
├── next.config.ts       # Static export configuration
└── package.json         # Next.js 15, React 19, SWR, MSW, shadcn/ui
```

**Key Features:**
- Static export ready (builds to `out/` directory for Go embedding)
- MSW intercepts all fetch calls in dev (can be removed to hit real backend)
- Fully typed API client matching `2026-02-24-api-design.md`
- Mock data includes all resources with realistic relationships
- Live activity feed with SSE simulation
- Real-time pipeline status visualization

### 6. Go Backend - Implementation Details

**Core Packages & Interfaces:**

| Package | Key Files | Status |
|---------|-----------|--------|
| `api/` | server.go, pgstore.go, projects.go, releases.go, sources.go, subscriptions.go, channels.go, events.go, health.go, middleware.go, auth.go, response.go | ✅ Complete |
| `ingestion/` | source.go, service.go, pgstore.go, orchestrator.go, loader.go, dockerhub.go, github.go | ✅ Complete |
| `pipeline/` | node.go, runner.go, worker.go, pgstore.go, regex_normalizer.go, subscription_router.go, urgency_scorer.go | ✅ Complete (core nodes) |
| `db/` | connections, migrations | ✅ Complete |
| `models/` | release.go, subscription.go, channel.go | ✅ Complete |
| `queue/` | River job queue setup | ✅ Complete |
| `routing/` | Notification routing | 🔴 Planned |
| `agents/` | LLM orchestration | 🔴 Planned |

**Main Entry Point** (`cmd/server/main.go`):
- Initializes PostgreSQL connection pool
- Runs database migrations
- Sets up pipeline runner with always-on nodes (Regex Normalizer, Subscription Router) and configurable nodes (Urgency Scorer)
- Creates River job client with pipeline worker
- Initializes ingestion service with Docker Hub poller and GitHub webhook handler
- Registers all API v1 routes with middleware chain
- Starts three background goroutines: polling orchestrator, PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY bridge, HTTP server
- Graceful shutdown with 10-second timeout

**Key Design Patterns:**
- Interface-based dependency injection for testability
- Transactional outbox pattern for zero-loss event processing
- PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY for real-time SSE updates
- River queue with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` for concurrent job processing
- Sequential pipeline with extensible node interface
- Configurable per-project pipeline via JSONB `pipeline_config`

---

### 7. Existing API Endpoints

All endpoints are fully implemented and documented:

**Projects:** GET, POST, GET {id}, PUT {id}, DELETE {id}
**Releases:** GET, GET {id}, GET {id}/pipeline, GET {id}/notes
**Subscriptions:** GET, POST, GET {id}, PUT {id}, DELETE {id}
**Sources:** GET, POST, GET {id}, PUT {id}, DELETE {id}, GET {id}/latest-release, GET {id}/releases/{version}
**Channels:** GET, POST, GET {id}, PUT {id}, DELETE {id}
**Providers:** GET (metadata)
**Health & Stats:** GET /api/v1/health (public), GET /api/v1/stats
**Events (SSE):** GET /api/v1/events (real-time)
**Webhooks:** POST /webhook/github (HMAC-SHA256)

**Authentication:** Bearer token via API keys (hashed in database)
**Rate Limiting:** 10 req/sec sustained, 20 req/sec burst per key
**Response Format:** Consistent envelope with `data`, `meta` (includes pagination, request_id)

---

### 8. Git Branches & Worktrees

**Branches:**
- `main` (current, clean working tree)
- `entire/checkpoints/v1` (default remote)
- `feat/ingestion-layer` (remote)
- `feat/integration-test` (remote)
- `feat/rest-api-layer` (remote)
- `feat/ux-layer` (remote + local worktree)

**Worktree:**
- `.worktrees/ux-layer` - Points to `feat/ux-layer` branch (prunable, checkout 3c52584)

---

### 9. Testing Coverage

Each major layer has comprehensive unit tests:
- **API layer:** auth, channels, events, health, middleware, projects, releases, response, sources, subscriptions
- **Ingestion layer:** dockerhub, github, orchestrator, service
- **Pipeline layer:** node, regex_normalizer, runner, subscription_router, urgency_scorer, worker

Tests use mocked dependencies (no real database required for unit tests).

---

### 10. Next Steps & Planned Work

Based on the implementation plans, the roadmap is:

1. **Routing Layer** - Implement `INotificationChannel` interface and notification matrix
2. **Agents Layer** - LLM integration (Gemini/GPT-4o-mini) for changelog analysis and SRE validation
3. **Advanced Pipeline Nodes** - Availability Checker, Risk Assessor, Adoption Tracker, Changelog Summarizer, Validation Trigger
4. **Real Backend Integration** - Replace MSW mocks with real API calls (remove MSW when backend is ready)
5. **Plugin System** - External node plugins via gRPC or HTTP callbacks
6. **Production Deployment** - Single binary build with embedded frontend

---

This is a well-architected, thoroughly planned system with substantial implementation already complete. The project demonstrates strong software engineering practices: interface-driven design, comprehensive documentation, test-first approach, and clear separation of concerns across layered architecture.
```

> AGENT

I've explored the project — ReleaseBeacon is in great shape with a fully implemented ingestion layer, pipeline (core nodes), REST API, and a complete Next.js frontend with MSW mocks. The remaining planned work includes the routing/notification layer, LLM agents, and advanced pipeline nodes.

What are you looking to build next? What idea or feature do you want to brainstorm?

> AGENT

This is a substantial rethink — moving from a pipeline-centric model to a project-centric, agent-driven model. Let me make sure I understand the core vision before we explore approaches.

The current design treats sources, subscriptions, and projects as loosely coupled entities with a fixed pipeline processing each release independently. You're proposing:

1. **Sources become children of projects** (not standalone)
2. **Cross-source intelligence** — detect the same release across Docker Hub, GitHub, etc. and correlate them
3. **Agent replaces fixed pipeline** — instead of sequential nodes, an LLM agent with tools gathers context, waits for signals from multiple sources, then produces a consolidated report
4. **Idle-based batching** — agent collects signals until quiet, then reports

This is a fundamentally different architecture. Let me start clarifying.

> AGENT

Now let me summarize what we've established and propose approaches.

---

## What We've Established

- **Project** = one upstream dependency (e.g., "PostgreSQL") with multiple sources (Docker Hub, GitHub, etc.)
- **Sources are children of projects**, not standalone entities
- **Agent per release event** — long-lived/resumable, accumulates signals from all sources
- **Idle window** — configurable per project, agent reports when sources go quiet
- **Cross-source linking** — semver normalization + temporal proximity, agent resolves ambiguity
- **Existing pipeline nodes become agent tools**
- **Report = stored artifact** — agent saves structured report to DB, UI displays it instantly

---

## Three Approaches

### Approach A: Agent-First (Recommended)

Replace the fixed pipeline entirely with an agent-per-release model.

**How it works:**
- New release detected → look for existing active agent for this project+version (via normalized semver + time window) → create or resume agent
- Agent has tools: `check_dockerhub`, `check_github_release`, `score_urgency`, `check_adoption`, `normalize_version`, `generate_report`
- Agent state persisted in DB as a JSON document (tool calls, findings, status)
- Idle timer tracked per agent — when it fires, agent produces final report and triggers notifications
- Report stored as structured JSON + markdown summary

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) Most aligned with your vision — mirrors how an engineer actually works
- (+) Flexible — agent can adapt its investigation based on what it finds
- (+) Natural batching — one agent per release collects all signals
- (-) LLM costs for every release, even routine ones
- (-) Non-deterministic — same input may produce different reports
- (-) More complex to test and debug

### Approach B: Hybrid — Deterministic Core + Agent Escalation

Keep deterministic processing for the common case, escalate to agent for complex situations.

**How it works:**
- New release → deterministic cross-source linking (semver + timing)
- Deterministic checks run first: version normalization, urgency scoring, availability check
- If all signals are clear (e.g., routine patch, all sources agree) → auto-generate report, no LLM needed
- If ambiguous (version mismatch, unusual timing, major version bump) → spawn agent for deeper investigation
- Agent has the same toolset as Approach A

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) Cost-efficient — most releases don't need an LLM
- (+) Deterministic for the common case — predictable, testable
- (+) Agent adds value where it matters most (complex/ambiguous cases)
- (-) Two code paths to maintain
- (-) Less "intelligent" for routine releases
- (-) Deciding when to escalate is its own complexity

### Approach C: Event Accumulator + Agent Reporter

Decouple signal collection from analysis entirely.

**How it works:**
- Sources feed events into an accumulator per project (pure data collection, no intelligence)
- Cross-source linking happens via deterministic rules during accumulation
- When idle timer fires → agent receives the full accumulated context and produces a report in one shot
- Agent is stateless — gets all context at report time, not during collection

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) Simplest agent integration — agent is a pure function (context in, report out)
- (+) Easy to test — accumulation is deterministic, agent is called once
- (+) Cheapest LLM usage — one call per release cycle
- (-) No real-time intelligence during accumulation
- (-) Agent can't guide its own investigation (can't decide to check something extra)
- (-) Less aligned with the "engineer hanging on the line" vision

---

**My recommendation is Approach A (Agent-First).** It's the most aligned with your vision of an engineer monitoring a release. The cost concern is manageable — most projects won't have releases every hour, and we can use a cheaper model (GPT-4o-mini) for routine checks. The non-determinism is actually a feature here: the agent can notice unexpected things a fixed pipeline would miss.

That said, Approach C is worth considering if you want to start simpler and evolve toward A later.

What do you think? Which approach resonates, or would you like to combine elements?

> DEVELOPER

But before the agent report, I still want to see the normal report and releases from each sources just based on the release notes, is that do-able?

> AGENT

Absolutely. That's essentially Approach C's accumulation layer + Approach A's agent on top.

The flow would be:

1. **Source detects new release** → immediately store it with whatever metadata the source provides (release notes, tags, timestamps, changelog)
2. **Deterministic processing runs instantly** — version normalization, cross-source linking, basic urgency score. This produces a **preliminary report** visible in the UI right away
3. **Agent is spawned/resumed** — it sees the preliminary data, uses tools to dig deeper (check binary availability, adoption metrics, etc.), accumulates signals from other sources
4. **Idle timer fires** → agent produces the **final enriched report**, replacing/augmenting the preliminary one

So in the UI, a release would have a status progression:

- **"New"** — raw release data from source, release notes visible immediately
- **"Investigating"** — agent is active, preliminary report available (deterministic scores + raw release notes)
- **"Complete"** — agent has produced the final report with cross-source correlation, availability checks, recommendations

You'd always see the per-source releases and their notes — the agent report is an enrichment layer on top, not a replacement.

> AGENT

Let me present the design section by section. I'll start with the data model since that's where the biggest changes land.

---

## Design Section 1: Data Model

### Projects (revised)

A project represents one upstream dependency. Sources are now embedded as children.

```
Project {
  id, name, description
  sources: []{              // nested, not a separate entity
    type: "dockerhub" | "github" | ...
    config: { repo, owner, image, ... }
    poll_interval: duration
  }
  settings: {
    idle_window: duration   // e.g., "6h" — how long to wait before final report
    agent_enabled: bool     // opt-in agent enrichment
    agent_model: string     // e.g., "gpt-4o-mini" (only if agent_enabled)
  }
  notification_channels: [channel_ids]   // where to send reports
}
```

### Releases (revised)

A release is always scoped to a project. Multiple source observations link to one release via cross-source correlation.

```
Release {
  id, project_id
  version: string           // normalized semver
  status: "new" | "investigating" | "complete"
  
  observations: []{         // one per source that detected this release
    source_type, source_config
    raw_version: string     // original tag/version string
    release_notes: text     // from the source
    detected_at: timestamp
    metadata: jsonb         // source-specific data (commit SHA, image digest, etc.)
  }
  
  // Deterministic report (always populated)
  urgency_score: float
  version_info: { major, minor, patch, pre_release, ... }
  
  // Agent report (optional, populated when agent_enabled + agent completes)
  agent_report: {
    summary: markdown
    availability: { docker: bool, binary: bool, ... }
    adoption: { ... }
    risk_assessment: text
    recommendations: []string
    completed_at: timestamp
  }
}
```

### Removed / Collapsed

- **Sources table** → embedded in projects (no standalone CRUD)
- **Subscriptions table** → replaced by `project.notification_channels` (direct link)
- **Pipeline jobs** → replaced by agent sessions (when agent is enabled)

Does this data model restructuring look right before I move to the next section?

> DEVELOPER

I think we can still keep the source table to store per source releases and release notes as an cache layer?
Subscrption table also need to be perserved as user can config and store them right?
Pipeline jobs can be removed

> AGENT

You're right on both counts.

- **Sources table** stays — it's the cache/storage layer for per-source raw data (tags, release notes, timestamps). It just becomes a child of project instead of standalone.
- **Subscriptions table** stays — users configure which projects route to which channels, with their own filter rules.
- **Pipeline jobs** removed — agent sessions replace them when enabled.

Let me revise:

---

## Design Section 1 (Revised): Data Model

### Projects (revised)

```
Project {
  id, name, description
  settings: {
    idle_window: duration       // default "6h"
    agent_enabled: bool         // opt-in
    agent_model: string         // only if agent_enabled
  }
}
```

### Sources (kept, now scoped to project)

```
Source {
  id, project_id              // FK to project — always belongs to a project
  type: "dockerhub" | "github" | ...
  config: { repo, owner, image, ... }
  poll_interval: duration
}
```

Each source stores its own raw releases:

### Source Releases (cache layer)

```
SourceRelease {
  id, source_id, project_id
  raw_version: string         // original tag string e.g., "REL_16_3"
  release_notes: text         // raw from source
  metadata: jsonb             // commit SHA, image digest, etc.
  detected_at: timestamp
}
```

### Releases (correlated, project-level)

```
Release {
  id, project_id
  version: string             // normalized semver
  status: "new" | "investigating" | "complete"
  
  source_release_ids: []      // links to SourceRelease rows that map to this release
  
  // Deterministic (always populated)
  urgency_score: float
  version_info: jsonb
  
  // Agent report (optional)
  agent_report: jsonb         // null when agent not enabled or not yet complete
}
```

### Subscriptions (kept)

```
Subscription {
  id, project_id, channel_id
  filters: jsonb              // e.g., min urgency, version patterns, etc.
  enabled: bool
}
```

### Removed
- **Pipeline jobs** → replaced by agent sessions (when agent_enabled)

So the hierarchy is: **Project → Sources → SourceReleases**, with **Releases** as the correlated project-level view linking back to SourceReleases. Subscriptions wire projects to notification channels.

```
  settings: {

  }
}

  type: "dockerhub" | "github" | ...
  config: jsonb                 // repo, owner, image, etc.
  // no poll_interval here — inherited from project
}
```

Server config:
```
AGENT_MODEL=gpt-4o-mini        // env var, applies globally
```

Now let me move to the processing design.

---

## Design Section 2: Processing Flow

### Two-layer architecture

**Layer 1: Deterministic (always runs)**

```
Source poll fires
  → fetch new tags/releases from upstream
  → store as SourceRelease (raw cache)
  → normalize version (existing regex normalizer logic)
  → attempt cross-source linking:
      1. Match by normalized semver within project
      2. Consider timing proximity (within idle_window)
      3. If match found → link to existing Release
      4. If no match → create new Release
  → compute urgency score (existing scorer logic)
  → Release status = "new", visible in UI immediately
```

At this point the user sees: per-source releases with notes, a correlated Release with urgency score and version info. No LLM involved.

**Layer 2: Agent enrichment (optional, when project.agent_enabled)**

```
New Release created or updated with new SourceRelease
  → check for existing active AgentSession for this Release
  → if none → spawn AgentSession, set idle timer
  → if exists → feed new signal, reset idle timer
  
Agent has tools:
  - check_source_releases(release_id) → get all linked SourceReleases
  - score_urgency(version_info) → existing scorer
  - check_availability(source, version) → poll for binary/image existence
  - check_adoption(source, version) → download stats, star counts
  - generate_report(findings) → produce structured summary
  
Idle timer fires:
  → agent produces final report (structured JSON + markdown)
  → stored in Release.agent_report
  → Release status = "complete"
  → trigger notifications via subscriptions
```

**When agent is disabled:** notifications trigger on the deterministic layer alone — Release created → urgency above threshold → notify via subscriptions.

## Design Section 3: UX

### Sidebar Navigation (simplified)

Current sidebar has: Dashboard, Projects, Releases, Sources, Subscriptions, Channels.

**Proposed:** Sources and Releases become project sub-views, not top-level.

```
Sidebar:
  Dashboard          — overview across all projects
  Projects           — list of tracked dependencies
  Channels           — notification channel management (Slack, PagerDuty, etc.)
  Settings           — global config
```

### Dashboard

- **Stats cards**: total projects, active agents, releases this week, pending notifications
- **Activity feed** (SSE): real-time stream of new releases and agent reports across all projects
- Click any release in the feed → navigates to that project's release view

### Project List Page

Table of all projects with columns: name, source count, latest release, urgency, agent status (enabled/disabled).

### Project Detail Page (the core view)

This is the most important page. Three sections:

**Header:** Project name, description, edit button, agent toggle

**Sources panel:** Collapsible list of configured sources (Docker Hub, GitHub, etc.) with add/remove

**Releases section:**
- Dropdown filter: "All sources" | "Docker Hub" | "GitHub" | ...
- Table/cards showing releases, each row showing:
  - Normalized version
  - Source badges (which sources detected it)
  - Urgency score
  - Status: New / Investigating / Complete
  - Detected timestamp
- **Click a release → popup/drawer** with:
  - Per-source release notes (tabs or accordion by source)
  - Deterministic report (urgency, version breakdown)
  - Agent report section (if enabled): summary, availability matrix, adoption, recommendations
  - If agent is still investigating: show progress/status

### Subscriptions

Managed within project detail — a "Notifications" tab showing which channels this project routes to, with filter config (min urgency, version patterns).

### Channels Page

Standalone — manage Slack webhooks, PagerDuty keys, etc. These are shared across projects.

## Design Section 4: Impact on Existing Code

### Backend Changes

**Keep & reuse:**
- `internal/ingestion/` — polling engine, Docker Hub source, GitHub webhook. Add `project_id` scoping
- `internal/pipeline/regex_normalizer.go` — version normalization becomes a utility/tool
- `internal/pipeline/urgency_scorer.go` — urgency scoring becomes a utility/tool
- `internal/api/` — handler structure, middleware, auth, SSE events. Restructure routes
- `internal/db/` — migrations, connection pooling
- `internal/models/` — update models to new schema

**Remove:**
- `internal/pipeline/runner.go`, `worker.go` — fixed pipeline runner replaced by agent
- `internal/pipeline/subscription_router.go` — routing logic moves to notification trigger
- Pipeline job tables and River pipeline worker

**Add new:**
- `internal/correlation/` — cross-source release linking (semver match + timing)
- `internal/agent/` — agent session management, tool definitions, idle timer, LLM client
- New DB tables: `source_releases`, `agent_sessions`
- Schema changes: `sources` gets `project_id` FK, `releases` gets `source_release_ids`, `agent_report`

### Frontend Changes

**Keep & reuse:**
- App shell, sidebar (simplify navigation items)
- shadcn/ui components, SWR data fetching, MSW mock layer
- Channel CRUD pages (mostly unchanged)
- Dashboard layout (update cards and feed)

**Restructure:**
- Project detail page becomes the main workhorse — absorbs sources, releases, subscriptions
- Source management moves inline into project create/edit
- Release list becomes a project sub-view with source dropdown filter
- Release detail becomes a popup/drawer instead of a separate page
- Remove standalone Sources and Subscriptions pages

**Add new:**
- Release popup with tabbed per-source notes + agent report
- Agent status indicators (investigating/complete)
- Source dropdown filter on project releases view
- Subscription management tab within project detail

```json
  "content": "# Project-Centric Redesign — Design Document\n\n**Date:** 2026-02-24\n**Status:** Approved\n\n## Summary\n\nRedesign ReleaseBeacon from a pipeline-centric model to a project-centric, two-layer architecture. A project represents one upstream dependency (e.g., \"PostgreSQL\") with multiple sources. Deterministic processing runs instantly on every release. Optional LLM agent enrichment provides deeper analysis when enabled per project.\n\n## Core Principles\n\n1. **Project = one dependency** — sources are children of projects, not standalone entities\n2. **Two-layer processing** — deterministic layer always works; agent enrichment is opt-in\n3. **Cross-source intelligence** — detect identical releases across sources and correlate them\n4. **Idle-based batching** — agent accumulates signals until configurable quiet period, then reports\n5. **Stored artifacts** — agent reports are persisted, not generated on-the-fly\n\n---\n\n## Data Model\n\n### Projects (revised)\n\n```\nProject {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  name            TEXT NOT NULL\n  description     TEXT\n  poll_interval   INTERVAL DEFAULT '15m'    -- applies to all sources\n  idle_window     INTERVAL DEFAULT '6h'     -- quiet period before agent reports\n  agent_enabled   BOOLEAN DEFAULT false     -- opt-in agent enrichment\n  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n}\n```\n\n### Sources (scoped to project)\n\n```\nSource {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  project_id      UUID REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  type            TEXT NOT NULL              -- \"dockerhub\", \"github\", etc.\n  config          JSONB NOT NULL            -- {repo, owner, image, ...}\n  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n}\n```\n\n### Source Releases (raw cache layer)\n\nPer-source raw release data. One row per source observation.\n\n```\nSourceRelease {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  source_id       UUID REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  project_id      UUID REFERENCES projects(id)\n  release_id      UUID REFERENCES releases(id)    -- nullable until correlated\n  raw_version     TEXT NOT NULL              -- original tag e.g., \"REL_16_3\"\n  release_notes   TEXT                       -- raw from upstream\n  metadata        JSONB                      -- commit SHA, image digest, etc.\n  detected_at     TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL\n  UNIQUE(source_id, raw_version)\n}\n```\n\n### Releases (correlated project-level view)\n\nOne row per unique release version within a project. Links to one or more SourceReleases.\n\n```\nRelease {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  project_id      UUID REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  version         TEXT NOT NULL              -- normalized semver\n  status          TEXT DEFAULT 'new'         -- \"new\" | \"investigating\" | \"complete\"\n  urgency_score   FLOAT\n  version_info    JSONB                      -- {major, minor, patch, pre_release, ...}\n  agent_report    JSONB                      -- null if agent not enabled or not yet complete\n  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  UNIQUE(project_id, version)\n}\n```\n\n### Subscriptions (kept)\n\n```\nSubscription {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  project_id      UUID REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  channel_id      UUID REFERENCES channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  filters         JSONB                      -- {min_urgency, version_patterns, ...}\n  enabled         BOOLEAN DEFAULT true\n  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n}\n```\n\n### Channels (unchanged)\n\nNotification endpoints (Slack webhook, PagerDuty, etc.). Shared across projects.\n\n### Agent Sessions (new, replaces pipeline_jobs)\n\n```\nAgentSession {\n  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY\n  release_id      UUID REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n  project_id      UUID REFERENCES projects(id)\n  status          TEXT DEFAULT 'active'      -- \"active\" | \"idle_waiting\" | \"complete\" | \"failed\"\n  tool_calls      JSONB                      -- ordered log of tool invocations and results\n  findings        JSONB                      -- accumulated context from tools\n  idle_deadline   TIMESTAMPTZ               -- when to produce final report\n  started_at      TIMESTAMPTZ\n  completed_at    TIMESTAMPTZ\n}\n```\n\n### Removed Tables\n\n- `pipeline_jobs` — replaced by `agent_sessions`\n\n### Server Configuration\n\n```\nAGENT_MODEL=gpt-4o-mini    # LLM model for agent enrichment (env var, global)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Processing Flow\n\n### Layer 1: Deterministic (always runs)\n\n```\nSource poll fires (per project.poll_interval)\n  -> fetch new tags/releases from upstream registry\n  -> store as SourceRelease (raw cache)\n  -> normalize version via regex normalizer (existing logic)\n  -> cross-source linking:\n      1. Match by normalized semver within same project\n      2. Consider temporal proximity (within project.idle_window)\n      3. Match found -> link SourceRelease to existing Release\n      4. No match -> create new Release\n  -> compute urgency score (existing scorer logic)\n  -> Release.status = \"new\"\n  -> visible in UI immediately\n  -> if NOT agent_enabled: trigger notifications via subscriptions\n```\n\n### Layer 2: Agent Enrichment (optional)\n\n```\nNew Release created or SourceRelease linked to existing Release\n  -> if NOT project.agent_enabled: skip\n  -> check for active AgentSession for this Release\n  -> if none: spawn AgentSession, set idle_deadline = now + project.idle_window\n  -> if exists: feed new signal to session, reset idle_deadline\n\nAgent tools (wrapping existing logic):\n  - check_source_releases(release_id)   -> all linked SourceReleases with notes\n  - normalize_version(raw_version)      -> existing regex normalizer\n  - score_urgency(version_info)         -> existing urgency scorer\n  - check_availability(source, version) -> poll for binary/image existence\n  - check_adoption(source, version)     -> download stats, star counts\n  - generate_report(findings)           -> produce structured JSON + markdown\n\nIdle timer fires:\n  -> agent produces final report\n  -> stored in Release.agent_report\n  -> Release.status = \"complete\"\n  -> trigger notifications via subscriptions\n```\n\n### Cross-Source Release Linking\n\nDetection strategy (in order):\n\n1. **Normalized semver match** — if two SourceReleases in the same project normalize to the same semver, they're the same release\n2. **Temporal proximity** — releases detected within the project's idle_window are candidates for matching\n3. **Agent resolution** — if agent_enabled and matching is ambiguous, the agent decides using context (changelogs, timing, metadata)\n\n---\n\n## UX Design\n\n### Sidebar Navigation (simplified)\n\n```\nDashboard           -- overview across all projects\nProjects            -- list of tracked dependencies\nChannels            -- notification channel management\nSettings            -- global configuration\n```\n\nSources, Releases, and Subscriptions are no longer top-level navigation items. They live within project views.\n\n### Dashboard\n\n- **Stats cards:** total projects, active agents, releases this week, pending notifications\n- **Activity feed (SSE):** real-time stream of new releases and agent reports\n- Click any item -> navigate to project release view\n\n### Project List Page\n\nTable: name, source count, latest release, urgency, agent enabled/disabled.\n\n### Project Detail Page (core view)\n\nThe main workhorse. Three sections:\n\n**Header:** Project name, description, edit, agent toggle, poll interval, idle window.\n\n**Sources panel:** Collapsible list of configured sources with type badges. Add/remove inline.\n\n**Releases section:**\n- **Dropdown filter:** \"All sources\" | per-source filters\n- **Release table/cards:**\n  - Normalized version\n  - Source badges (which sources detected it)\n  - Urgency score\n  - Status badge: New / Investigating / Complete\n  - Detected timestamp\n- **Click release -> popup/drawer:**\n  - Per-source release notes (tabs or accordion by source)\n  - Deterministic report: urgency, version breakdown\n  - Agent report section (if enabled):\n    - Summary (markdown)\n    - Availability matrix (docker, binary, etc.)\n    - Adoption metrics\n    - Risk assessment\n    - Recommendations\n  - If agent still investigating: progress indicator\n\n**Notifications tab:** Subscriptions for this project — which channels, filter rules.\n\n### Project Create/Edit\n\n- Name, description, poll interval, idle window, agent toggle\n- Sources: inline list with add/remove, type selector, config fields per type\n\n### Channels Page (standalone)\n\nUnchanged — manage Slack webhooks, PagerDuty keys, etc. Shared across projects.\n\n---\n\n## Impact on Existing Code\n\n### Backend — Keep & Reuse\n\n| Package | What to keep | Changes needed |\n|---------|-------------|----------------|\n| `internal/ingestion/` | Polling engine, DockerHub source, GitHub webhook | Add `project_id` scoping to sources |\n| `internal/pipeline/regex_normalizer.go` | Version normalization logic | Extract as utility/tool callable by agent |\n| `internal/pipeline/urgency_scorer.go` | Urgency scoring logic | Extract as utility/tool callable by agent |\n| `internal/api/` | Handler structure, middleware, auth, SSE | Restructure routes, update handlers |\n| `internal/db/` | Migrations, connection pooling | New migration for schema changes |\n| `internal/models/` | Model patterns | Update to new schema |\n\n### Backend — Remove\n\n| What | Why |\n|------|-----|\n| `internal/pipeline/runner.go` | Fixed pipeline runner replaced by agent |\n| `internal/pipeline/worker.go` | River pipeline worker no longer needed |\n| `internal/pipeline/subscription_router.go` | Routing moves to notification trigger |\n| Pipeline job DB table | Replaced by `agent_sessions` |\n\n### Backend — Add New\n\n| Package | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `internal/correlation/` | Cross-source release linking (semver + timing) |\n| `internal/agent/` | Agent session management, tool definitions, idle timer, LLM client |\n| DB tables | `source_releases`, `agent_sessions` |\n| Schema changes | `sources.project_id` FK, `releases.agent_report` column |\n\n### Frontend — Keep & Reuse\n\n- App shell, sidebar layout (simplify nav items)\n- shadcn/ui components, SWR, MSW mock layer\n- Channel CRUD pages (mostly unchanged)\n- Dashboard layout (update stats cards and activity feed)\n\n### Frontend — Restructure\n\n- Project detail page becomes main workhorse (absorbs sources, releases, subscriptions)\n- Source management moves inline into project create/edit\n- Release list becomes project sub-view with source dropdown filter\n- Release detail becomes popup/drawer (not standalone page)\n- Remove standalone Sources, Subscriptions, and Releases pages\n\n### Frontend — Add New\n\n- Release popup/drawer with tabbed per-source notes + agent report\n- Agent status indicators (investigating / complete)\n- Source dropdown filter on project releases\n- Subscription management tab within project detail\n"
}
```

## 4. API CLIENT (`web/lib/api/client.ts`, lines 1-152)

**Base:** `/api/v1`

**request()** helper (lines 21-31): Generic fetch wrapper with error handling

### API Endpoints

**projects**
- `list(page, perPage)` → GET /projects?page=X&per_page=Y
- `get(id)` → GET /projects/{id}
- `create(input)` → POST /projects
- `update(id, input)` → PUT /projects/{id}
- `delete(id)` → DELETE /projects/{id}

**releases**
- `list(params: ListReleasesParams)` → GET /releases with query filters
  - Supports: project_id, source_id, pre_release, page, per_page, sort, order
- `get(id)` → GET /releases/{id}
- `pipeline(id)` → GET /releases/{id}/pipeline
- `notes(id)` → GET /releases/{id}/notes

**sources**
- `list()` → GET /sources
- `get(id)` → GET /sources/{id}
- `create(input)` → POST /sources
- `update(id, input)` → PUT /sources/{id}
- `delete(id)` → DELETE /sources/{id}
- `latestRelease(id)` → GET /sources/{id}/latest-release

**subscriptions**
- `list()` → GET /subscriptions
- `get(id)` → GET /subscriptions/{id}
- `create(input)` → POST /subscriptions
- `update(id, input)` → PUT /subscriptions/{id}
- `delete(id)` → DELETE /subscriptions/{id}

**channels**
- `list()` → GET /channels
- `get(id)` → GET /channels/{id}
- `create(input)` → POST /channels
- `update(id, input)` → PUT /channels/{id}
- `delete(id)` → DELETE /channels/{id}

**system**
- `health()` → GET /health
- `stats()` → GET /stats
- `providers()` → GET /providers

## 6. MOCK HANDLERS (`web/lib/mock/handlers.ts`, lines 1-265)

MSW (Mock Service Worker) handlers for all CRUD operations:

- Subscriptions: 5 handlers (list, post, get, put, delete)

### Dashboard Components

**StatsCards** (`web/components/dashboard/stats-cards.tsx`, lines 1-35)
- 4 cards: Total Releases, Active Sources, Pending Jobs, Failed Jobs
- Uses system.stats() API
- Icons from lucide-react

**RecentReleases** (`web/components/dashboard/recent-releases.tsx`, lines 1-67)
- Lists 8 most recent releases
- Shows project, version, source, status
- Links to full releases page

**ActivityFeed** (`web/components/dashboard/activity-feed.tsx`, lines 1-87)
- Live event stream from mockSSE
- Event type → color mapping
- Displays last 20 events
- Animated "live" indicator

### Project Components

**ProjectForm** (`web/components/projects/project-form.tsx`, lines 1-88)
- Fields: name, description, url, pipeline_config (JSON textarea)
- Props: initial, onSubmit, title
- Handles JSON validation

**ProjectDetail** (`web/components/projects/project-detail.tsx`, lines 1-134)
- Shows project name, description, URL (external link)
- Two-column grid:
  - Left: Pipeline Configuration breakdown
  - Right: Associated Sources list
- Edit/Delete buttons
- Link to add source

**ProjectEdit** (`web/components/projects/project-edit.tsx`, lines 1-20)
- Wrapper using SWR to load initial data
- Passes to ProjectForm with update handler

### Release Components

**ReleaseDetail** (`web/components/releases/release-detail.tsx`, lines 1-111)
- Three-column layout:
  - Left: Version details (semantic version, metadata), Release notes
  - Right: PipelineVisualization
- Shows release metadata, project link, source type, repository
- Pulls data from GET /releases/{id}, /releases/{id}/pipeline, /releases/{id}/notes

**PipelineVisualization** (`web/components/releases/pipeline-status.tsx`, lines 1-110)
- Displays pipeline state badge with icon
- Lists completed nodes with their results
- Shows current node if running
- Attempt counter
- Node name mapping (regex_normalizer → Regex Normalizer)

### Source Components

**SourceForm** (`web/components/sources/source-form.tsx`, lines 1-115)
- Fields: project (select), type (select from providers), repository, poll_interval_seconds, exclude_version_regexp, exclude_prereleases (switch), enabled (switch)
- Props: initial, defaultProjectId, onSubmit, title
- Loads projects and providers on mount

**SourceEdit** (`web/components/sources/source-edit.tsx`, lines 1-37)
- Wrapper with SWR + delete button
- Passes initial data to SourceForm

### Subscription Components

**SubscriptionForm** (`web/components/subscriptions/subscription-form.tsx`, lines 1-123)
- Fields: project (select), channel_type (select), channel_id (select), version_pattern, frequency (select), enabled (switch)
- Loads projects and channels on mount

**SubscriptionEdit** (`web/components/subscriptions/subscription-edit.tsx`, lines 1-37)
- Wrapper with SWR + delete button

### Channel Components

**ChannelForm** (`web/components/channels/channel-form.tsx`, lines 1-105)
- Fields: name, type (select: slack/pagerduty/webhook), enabled (switch)
- Dynamic config fields based on channel type:
  - **Slack:** webhook_url, channel
  - **PagerDuty:** routing_key
  - **Webhook:** url, headers
- Config key generation: label → lowercase with underscores

**ChannelEdit** (`web/components/channels/channel-edit.tsx`, lines 1-37)
- Wrapper with SWR + delete button

- button, card, input, label, select, textarea, switch, table, tabs, badge, dialog, dropdown-menu, separator, sheet, tooltip

## 11. KEY PATTERNS & ARCHITECTURE

### Data Fetching
- **SWR** for client-side data fetching with caching
- Key pattern: `[entity, filters...]` for revalidation
- Pagination: `page` and `per_page` parameters

### Forms
- Controlled components with useState
- Error handling with try/catch
- Redirect on success via `useRouter().push()`
- Delete confirmations with native `confirm()`

### Dynamic Content
- URL parameters: `[id]` routes use `params` promise (Next.js 15+)
- Search params: `useSearchParams()` for query strings (e.g., project_id)
- Static export workaround: `generateStaticParams()` returns placeholder `[{ id: "0" }]`

### Real-time Features
- MockSSE emits events every 5 seconds
- ActivityFeed subscribes and displays latest 20
- Discriminated union types for type-safe event handling

### Error States
- Loading spinners
- Error messages in red banners
- "Not found" fallback UI

**Schema Definition** (Lines 12-115):

#### Tables Created:

**`projects`** (Lines 18-26)
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS projects (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    description TEXT,
    url VARCHAR(500),
    pipeline_config JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{"changelog_summarizer": {}, "urgency_scorer": {}}'::jsonb,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
```

**`sources`** (Lines 29-43)
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sources (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    source_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    poll_interval_seconds INT DEFAULT 300,
    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    exclude_version_regexp TEXT,
    exclude_prereleases BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
    last_polled_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    UNIQUE(source_type, repository)
);
```

**`releases`** (Lines 46-53)
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS releases (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    source_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES sources(id),
    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    payload JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    UNIQUE(source_id, version)
);
```

**`pipeline_jobs`** (Lines 56-68)
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pipeline_jobs (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    state VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'available',
    release_id UUID REFERENCES releases(id),
    current_node VARCHAR(50),
    node_results JSONB DEFAULT '{}',
    attempt INT DEFAULT 0,
    max_attempts INT DEFAULT 3,
    error_message TEXT,
    locked_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

**`notification_channels`** (Lines 71-78)
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_channels (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
```

**`subscriptions`** (Lines 81-91)

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    channel_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    channel_id INT REFERENCES notification_channels(id),
    version_pattern TEXT,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
```


**Methods**:

- `ProcessResults(ctx context.Context, sourceID int, sourceName string, results []IngestionResult) error` (Line 22)
  - Normalizes raw results to ReleaseEvent IR
  - Calls `store.IngestRelease()` for each result

#### PgStore (`pgstore.go`):

**PgStore** (Lines 16-18):
```go
type PgStore struct {
    pool  *pgxpool.Pool
    river *river.Client[pgx.Tx]
}
```

**Methods**:
- `NewPgStore(pool *pgxpool.Pool, riverClient *river.Client[pgx.Tx]) *PgStore` (Line 21)
- `IngestRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceID int, event *models.ReleaseEvent) error` (Line 27)
  - **Transactional Outbox Pattern**: 
    - Inserts release to `releases` table
    - Enqueues pipeline job via `river.InsertTx()` 
    - Both happen in single TX; both rollback on error
    - Line 47: `s.river.InsertTx(ctx, tx, queue.PipelineJobArgs{ReleaseID: event.ID}, nil)`

- `Execute(ctx context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent, _ json.RawMessage, _ map[string]json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error)` (Line 30)
  - Regex pattern (Line 13): `^v?(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?(?:\.(\d+))?(?:[+-]([\w.]+))?$`
  - Parses semantic version from `RawVersion`
  - Detects pre-releases if contains `[+-]suffix`
  - Mutates event's `SemanticVersion` and `IsPreRelease` fields
  - Returns JSON-marshaled `RegexNormalizerResult`
  - **Always-on node** — runs unconditionally

#### Urgency Scorer (`urgency_scorer.go`):

```go
}
```

**UrgencyScorer** (Line 22):
```go
type UrgencyScorer struct{}
```

**Scoring Logic** (Lines 28-64):
- Security keywords (Line 12): "security", "cve", "vulnerability", "critical", "exploit", "rce", "injection"
- If security keyword found in changelog → **CRITICAL** score
- Else if pre-release → **LOW**
- Else if major version (minor==0, patch==0) → **HIGH**
- Else if patch version (patch > 0) → **LOW**
- Else (minor version) → **MEDIUM** (default)

**Methods**:
- `NewUrgencyScorer() *UrgencyScorer` (Line 24)
- `Name() string` (Line 26) — Returns "urgency_scorer"
- `Execute(ctx context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent, _ json.RawMessage, prior map[string]json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error)` (Line 28)
  - **Configurable node** — only runs if enabled in `pipeline_config`
  - Reads prior results from `regex_normalizer` node

**Methods**:
- `NewRunner(store Store, alwaysOn []PipelineNode, configurable []PipelineNode) *Runner` (Line 25)
- `Process(ctx context.Context, releaseID string, pipelineConfig map[string]json.RawMessage) error` (Line 32)
  - Loads release payload from store
  - Creates pipeline job in "running" state
  - Executes all always-on nodes first (Line 46)
  - Then executes configurable nodes that are enabled in pipelineConfig (Line 57)
  - Stops on `ErrEventDropped` or other errors
  - On completion, calls `store.CompletePipelineJob(ctx, jobID, nodeResults)`
- `runNode(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, node PipelineNode, event *models.ReleaseEvent, config json.RawMessage, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) (stopped bool, err error)` (Line 79)
  - Records progress before execution
  - On `ErrEventDropped` → marks job as "skipped", returns (true, nil)
  - On error → marks job as "failed", returns (false, err)
  - On success → accumulates result in nodeResults map

#### Store Interface (`store.go`):

**Store** (Lines 12-32):
```go
type Store interface {
    // GetReleasePayload loads the ReleaseEvent IR from the releases table.
    GetReleasePayload(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (*models.ReleaseEvent, error)

    // CreatePipelineJob inserts a new pipeline_jobs row in "running" state
    // and returns the generated job ID.
    CreatePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (int64, error)

    // UpdateNodeProgress records the current node and accumulated results
    // while the pipeline is still running.
    UpdateNodeProgress(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, currentNode string, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error

    // CompletePipelineJob marks the job as "completed" with final node results.
    CompletePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error

    // SkipPipelineJob marks the job as "skipped" (e.g., event dropped by a node).
    SkipPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, reason string) error

    // FailPipelineJob marks the job as "failed" with an error message.
    FailPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, errMsg string) error
}
```

**SubscriptionChecker** (Lines 34-40):
```go
type SubscriptionChecker interface {
    // HasSubscribers returns true if the repository has at least one enabled
    // subscription, or if no subscriptions exist at all ("default open" behavior).
    HasSubscribers(ctx context.Context, repository string) (bool, error)
}
```

#### Client (`queue/client.go`):

**NewRiverClient** (Lines 12-21):
```go
func NewRiverClient(pool *pgxpool.Pool, workers *river.Workers) (*river.Client[pgx.Tx], error) {
    config := &river.Config{}
    if workers != nil {
        config.Workers = workers
        config.Queues = map[string]river.QueueConfig{
            river.QueueDefault: {MaxWorkers: 100},
        }
    }
    return river.NewClient(riverpgxv5.New(pool), config)
}
```

### 7. MAIN ENTRY POINT

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go`

**Startup Sequence** (Lines 19-134):

1. **Environment setup** (Lines 23-25)
   - DATABASE_URL, GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET, LISTEN_ADDR

2. **Database initialization** (Lines 28-38)
   - Create connection pool
   - Run migrations (idempotent)

3. **Pipeline setup** (Lines 41-51)
   - Create PgStore
   - Create Runner with always-on nodes: RegexNormalizer, SubscriptionRouter
   - Create Runner with configurable nodes: UrgencyScorer
   - Pattern: `pipeline.NewRunner(store, alwaysOn, configurable)`

4. **River queue setup** (Lines 54-66)
   - Create Workers registry
   - Add PipelineWorker to registry
   - Create River client with workers (MaxWorkers: 100)
   - Start River client

5. **Ingestion setup** (Lines 69-87)
   - Create SourceLoader
   - Create Service
   - Create Orchestrator with 5-minute poll interval
   - Create GitHub webhook handler with callback
   - Webhook callback: looks up source by (type, repo) and calls Service.ProcessResults

6. **API setup** (Lines 90-109)
   - Create PgStore
   - Create Broadcaster
   - Register all routes
   - All dependencies passed via Dependencies struct

7. **Server startup** (Lines 111-125)
   - Create HTTP server
   - Start polling orchestrator in background goroutine
   - Start PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY → SSE bridge in background goroutine
   - Start HTTP server in background goroutine
   - Wait for interrupt signal
   - Graceful shutdown (10-second timeout)

1: Write failing test for version normalization utility**\n\n```go\n// internal/correlation/version_test.go\nfunc TestParseVersion(t *testing.T) {\n    tests := []struct {\n        raw     string\n models.SemanticData{Major: 1, Minor: 2, Patch: 3, PreRelease: \"rc1\"}, true, true},\n        {\"REL_16_3\", models.SemanticData{}, false, false}, // won't match standard regex\n    }\n    for _, tt := range tests {\n        sem, isPre, ok := ParseVersion(tt.raw)\n        // assertions\n    }\n}\n```\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/correlation/... -v` — expect FAIL\n\n**Step 2: Implement ParseVersion**\n\nMove and refactor regex normalizer logic into `internal/correlation/version.go`.\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/correlation/... -v` — expect PASS\n\n**Step 3: Write failing test for correlator**\n\nThe correlator's job: given a new SourceRelease, find or create the matching project-scoped Release.\n\n```go\n// internal/correlation/correlator_test.go\nfunc TestCorrelator_Correlate(t *testing.T) {\n    // Test 1: new version creates new Release\n    // Test 2: same normalized version links to existing Release\n    // Test 3: different raw versions that normalize to same semver get linked\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Implement Correlator**\n\n```go\n// internal/correlation/correlator.go\ntype CorrelationStore interface {\n    FindReleaseByVersion(ctx context.Context, projectID int, version string) (*models.Release, error)\n    CreateRelease(ctx context.Context, r *models.Release) error\n    LinkSourceRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceReleaseID string, releaseID string) error\n}\n\ntype Correlator struct {\n    store CorrelationStore\n}\n\nfunc NewCorrelator(store CorrelationStore) *Correlator {\n    return &Correlator{store: store}\n}\n\n// Correlate takes a new SourceRelease and returns the correlated Release (found or created).\nfunc (c *Correlator) Correlate(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SourceRelease) (*models.Release, error) {\n    // 1. Parse version\n    sem, isPre, ok := ParseVersion(sr.RawVersion)\n\n    // 2. Build normalized version string\n    var version string\n    if ok {\n        version = sem.String()\n    } else {\n        version = sr.RawVersion // fallback to raw\n    }\n\n    // 3. Look for existing release with same project + version\n    existing, err := c.store.FindReleaseByVersion(ctx, sr.ProjectID, version)\n    if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {\n        return nil, err\n    }\n\n    if existing != nil {\n        // Link this source release to existing\n        c.store.LinkSourceRelease(ctx, sr.ID, existing.ID)\n        return existing, nil\n    }\n\n    // 4. Create new release\n    score, factors := ScoreUrgency(sem, isPre, sr.ReleaseNotes)\n    release := &models.Release{\n        ProjectID:    sr.ProjectID,\n        Version:      version,\n        Status:       \"new\",\n        UrgencyScore: &urgencyFloat, // convert score string to float\n        VersionInfo:  marshalVersionInfo(sem, isPre, factors),\n    }\n    err = c.store.CreateRelease(ctx, release)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, err\n    }\n\n    c.store.LinkSourceRelease(ctx, sr.ID, release.ID)\n    return release, nil\n}\n```\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/correlation/... -v` — expect PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/correlation/\ngit commit -m \"feat: add correlation engine for cross-source release linking\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 5: Update ingestion layer for new schema\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `internal/ingestion/service.go`\n- Modify: `internal/ingestion/pgstore.go`\n- Modify: `internal/ingestion/store.go`\n- Modify: `internal/ingestion/orchestrator.go`\n- Modify: `internal/ingestion/loader.go`\n- Update tests\n\nThe ingestion layer now:\n1. Stores raw data as `SourceRelease` (instead of directly into `releases`)\n2. Calls the `Correlator` to link source releases to project-scoped releases\n3. No longer enqueues River pipeline jobs\n\n**Step 1: Update ReleaseStore interface**\n\n```go\n// internal/ingestion/store.go\ntype ReleaseStore interface {\n    IngestSourceRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceID int, projectID int, result *IngestionResult) (*models.SourceRelease, error)\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Update PgStore**\n\n```go\n// internal/ingestion/pgstore.go\nfunc (s *PgStore) IngestSourceRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceID int, projectID int, result *IngestionResult) (*models.SourceRelease, error) {\n    sr := &models.SourceRelease{}\n    err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx,\n        `INSERT INTO source_releases (source_id, project_id, raw_version, release_notes, metadata, detected_at)\n         VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6)\n         RETURNING id, source_id, project_id, raw_version, release_notes, metadata, detected_at`,\n        sourceID, projectID, result.RawVersion, result.Changelog,\n        metadataJSON(result.Metadata), result.Timestamp,\n    ).Scan(&sr.ID, &sr.SourceID, &sr.ProjectID, &sr.RawVersion, &sr.ReleaseNotes, &sr.Metadata, &sr.DetectedAt)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, err\n    }\n    return sr, nil\n}\n```\n\nThe PgStore no longer needs a River client. Remove that dependency.\n\n**Step 3: Update Service to use correlator**\n\n```go\n// internal/ingestion/service.go\ntype Service struct {\n    store      ReleaseStore\n    correlator *correlation.Correlator\n}\n\nfunc (s *Service) ProcessResults(ctx context.Context, sourceID, projectID int, sourceName string, results []IngestionResult) error {\n    for _, r := range results {\n        // 1. Store as SourceRelease\n        sr, err := s.store.IngestSourceRelease(ctx, sourceID, projectID, &r)\n        if err != nil {\n            // Skip duplicates (unique constraint violation)\n            continue\n        }\n        // 2. Correlate to project-level Release\n        _, err = s.correlator.Correlate(ctx, sr)\n        if err != nil {\n            log.Printf(\"correlation error: %v\", err)\n        }\n    }\n    return nil\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Update Orchestrator to pass projectID**\n\nThe orchestrator needs to know the project_id for each source. Update `IIngestionSource` to include `ProjectID()`:\n\n```go\ntype IIngestionSource interface {\n    Name() string\n    SourceID() int\n    ProjectID() int  // NEW\n    FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error)\n}\n```\n\nUpdate `DockerHubSource` and `GitHubWebhookHandler` accordingly.\n\nUpdate `SourceLoader.LoadEnabledSources()` to read project_id from the sources query and pass it to source constructors.\n\n**Step 5: Update orchestrator's pollAll**\n\n```go\nfunc (o *Orchestrator) pollAll(ctx context.Context) {\n    sources, err := o.loader.LoadEnabledSources(ctx)\n    // ...\n    for _, src := range sources {\n        results, err := src.FetchNewReleases(ctx)\n        // ...\n        o.service.ProcessResults(ctx, src.SourceID(), src.ProjectID(), src.Name(), results)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Step 6: Update tests**\n\nUpdate `internal/ingestion/service_test.go`, `orchestrator_test.go` to use new interface.\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/ingestion/... -v`\n\n**Step 7: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/ingestion/\ngit commit -m \"feat: update ingestion layer to store source_releases and use correlator\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 6: Update main.go\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `cmd/server/main.go`\n\n**Step 1: Remove pipeline and River setup**\n\nRemove:\n- Pipeline runner creation\n- River workers and client\n- Pipeline worker registration\n\n**Step 2: Add correlation setup**\n\n```go\n// Create correlation store + correlator\ncorrStore := correlation.NewPgStore(pool)\ncorrelator := correlation.NewCorrelator(corrStore)\n\n// Create ingestion service with correlator\ningestionStore := ingestion.NewPgStore(pool) // no River client\ningestionService := ingestion.NewService(ingestionStore, correlator)\n```\n\n**Step 3: Simplify startup**\n\nThe server now starts: DB → migrations → correlator → ingestion → API → HTTP.\n\nRun: `go build ./cmd/server`\n\n**Step 4: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add cmd/server/main.go\ngit commit -m \"refactor: simplify main.go, remove pipeline/River, add correlator\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: API Restructure\n\n### Task 7: Update API store interfaces and pgstore\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `internal/api/projects.go` — update ProjectsStore interface\n- Modify: `internal/api/releases.go` — rewrite *models.Project) error\n    GetProject(ctx context.Context, id int) (*models.Project, error)\n    UpdateProject(ctx context.Context, id int, p *models.Project) error\n    DeleteProject(ctx context.Context, id int) error\n    // NEW: get sources for a project\n    ListProjectSources(ctx context.Context, projectID int) ([]models.Source, error)\n    // NEW: get releases for a project\n    ListProjectReleases(ctx context.Context, projectID int, page, perPage int) ([]ProjectRelease, int, error)\n    // NEW: get a release with its source_releases\n    GetProjectRelease(ctx context.Context, projectID int, releaseID string) (*ProjectReleaseDetail, error)\n}\n```\n\nWhere `ProjectRelease` is a view model:\n\n```go\ntype ProjectRelease struct {\n    ID           string   `json:\"id\"`\n    Version      string   `json:\"version\"`\n    Status       string   `json:\"status\"`\n    UrgencyScore *float64 `json:\"urgency_score,omitempty\"`\n    SourceCount  int      `json:\"source_count\"`     // how many sources detected this\n    SourceTypes  []string `json:\"source_types\"`     // [\"dockerhub\", \"github\"]\n    CreatedAt    string   `json:\"created_at\"`\n}\n\ntype ProjectReleaseDetail struct {\n    ProjectRelease\n    VersionInfo    json.RawMessage       `json:\"version_info,omitempty\"`\n    AgentReport    json.RawMessage       `json:\"agent_report,omitempty\"`\n    SourceReleases []models.SourceRelease `json:\"source_releases\"` // per-source raw data\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Update route structure**\n\nRemove standalone `/api/v1/releases` and `/api/v1/sources` top-level routes.\nAdd project-nested routes:\n\n```\nGET    /api/v1/projects                           — list projects\nPOST   /api/v1/projects                           — create project\nGET    /api/v1/projects/{id}                       — get project (includes sources)\nPUT    /api/v1/projects/{id}                       — update project\nDELETE /api/v1/projects/{id}                       — delete project\nGET    /api/v1/projects/{id}/sources               — list sources for project\nPOST   /api/v1/projects/{id}/sources               — add source to project\nPUT    /api/v1/projects/{id}/sources/{sourceId}    — update source\nDELETE /api/v1/projects/{id}/sources/{sourceId}    — remove source\nGET    /api/v1/projects/{id}/releases              — list releases for project\nGET    /api/v1/projects/{id}/releases/{releaseId}  — get release detail (with source_releases)\n\nGET    /api/v1/channels                            — unchanged\nPOST   /api/v1/channels                            — unchanged\nGET    /api/v1/channels/{id}                       — unchanged\nPUT    /api/v1/channels/{id}                       — unchanged\nDELETE /api/v1/channels/{id}                       — unchanged\n\nGET    /api/v1/subscriptions                       — keep (may filter by project_id)\nPOST   /api/v1/subscriptions                       — keep\nGET    /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}                  — keep\nPUT    /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}                  — keep\nDELETE /api/v1/subscriptions/{id}                  — keep\n\nGET    /api/v1/health                              — unchanged\nGET    /api/v1/stats                               — update stats\nGET    /api/v1/events                              — unchanged\nGET    /api/v1/providers                           — unchanged\nPOST   /webhook/github                             — unchanged\n```\n\n**Step 3: Implement pgstore methods for new queries**\n\nKey new queries:\n\n```sql\n-- ListProjectReleases\nSELECT r.id, r.version, r.status, r.urgency_score, r.created_at,\n       COUNT(sr.id) as source_count,\n       ARRAY_AGG(DISTINCT s.source_type) as source_types\nFROM releases r\nLEFT JOIN source_releases sr ON sr.release_id = r.id\nLEFT JOIN sources s ON s.id = sr.source_id\nWHERE r.project_id = $1\nGROUP BY r.id\nORDER BY r.created_at DESC\nLIMIT $2 OFFSET $3\n\n-- GetProjectReleaseDetail\n-- Release + all linked source_releases\nSELECT r.*, sr.id, sr.source_id, sr.raw_version, sr.release_notes, sr.metadata, sr.detected_at\nFROM releases r\nLEFT JOIN source_releases sr ON sr.release_id = r.id\nWHERE r.id = $1 AND r.project_id = $2\n```\n\n**Step 4: Update pgstore project queries**\n\nUpdate `ListProjects` to include source count:\n```sql\nSELECT p.*, COUNT(s.id) as source_count\nFROM projects p\nLEFT JOIN sources s ON s.project_id = p.id\nGROUP BY p.id\nORDER BY p.created_at DESC\n```\n\nUpdate `CreateProject` / `UpdateProject` to handle new columns (poll_interval, idle_window, agent_enabled). Remove `pipeline_config`.\n\n**Step 5: Remove old releases/sources handler files or gut them**\n\n- `internal/api/releases.go` — Rewrite handlers for project-nested routes\n- `internal/api/sources.go` — Rewrite handlers for project-nested routes\n- Remove pipeline-related views from releases (PipelineStatus, pipeline endpoint)\n\n**Step 6: Update server.go RegisterRoutes**\n\nRemove old top-level release/source routes.      1→# Project-Centric Redesign Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Restructure ReleaseBeacon from pipeline-centric to project-centric architecture with two-layer processing (deterministic + optional agent enrichment).
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Sources become children of projects. A new `source_releases` table caches raw per-source data. The `releases` table becomes project-scoped with cross-source correlation. The fixed pipeline is removed in favor of deterministic processing + optional agent enrichment. The frontend consolidates sources, releases, and subscriptions into the project detail page.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** Go 1.25, PostgreSQL, Next.js 15, React 19, Tailwind CSS v4, SWR, MSW, shadcn/ui
    10→
    11→**Design Doc:** `docs/plans/2026-02-24-project-centric-redesign-design.md`
    12→
    13→---
    14→
    15→## Phase 1: Database Schema & Models
    16→
    17→### Task 1: Write migration for schema changes
    18→
    19→**Files:**
    20→- Modify: `internal/db/migrations.go`
    21→
    22→**Step 1: Read current migration file**
    23→
    24→Read `internal/db/migrations.go` to understand the full schema.
    25→
    26→**Step 2: Write the new migration SQL**
    27→
    28→Add a new migration block after the existing schema. The migration must be idempotent (use `IF NOT EXISTS`, `IF EXISTS`).
    29→
    30→Changes needed:
    31→
    32→```sql
    33→-- 1. Add new columns to projects
    34→ALTER TABLE projects ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS poll_interval INTERVAL DEFAULT '15 minutes';
    35→ALTER TABLE projects ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS idle_window INTERVAL DEFAULT '6 hours';
    36→ALTER TABLE projects ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS agent_enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;
    37→
    38→-- 2. Create source_releases table (raw cache layer)
    39→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS source_releases (
    40→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    41→    source_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    42→    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    43→    release_id UUID REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    44→    raw_version TEXT NOT NULL,
    45→    release_notes TEXT,
    46→    metadata JSONB DEFAULT '{}',
    47→    detected_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    48→    UNIQUE(source_id, raw_version)
    49→);
    50→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_source_releases_project ON source_releases(project_id);
    51→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_source_releases_release ON source_releases(release_id);
    52→
    53→-- 3. Restructure releases table
    54→-- We need to migrate releases from source-scoped to project-scoped.
    55→-- Strategy: add new columns, migrate data, then drop old constraints.
    56→
    57→-- Add new columns
    58→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS project_id INT REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE;
    59→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS status TEXT DEFAULT 'new';
    60→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS urgency_score FLOAT;
    61→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS version_info JSONB;
    62→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS agent_report JSONB;
    63→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS version TEXT;
    64→ALTER TABLE releases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW();
    65→
    66→-- Backfill project_id from sources
    67→UPDATE releases SET project_id = s.project_id FROM sources s WHERE releases.source_id = s.id AND releases.project_id IS NULL;
    68→
    69→-- Backfill version from payload (the raw_version field in the JSONB payload)
    70→UPDATE releases SET version = payload->>'raw_version' WHERE version IS NULL;
    71→
    72→-- Make project_id NOT NULL after backfill
    73→ALTER TABLE releases ALTER COLUMN project_id SET NOT NULL;
    74→
    75→-- Add new unique constraint (project_id, version)
    76→-- Drop old unique constraint first
    77→ALTER TABLE releases DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS releases_source_id_version_key;
    78→-- Create new one (use DO block for idempotency)
    79→DO $$ BEGIN
    80→    IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint WHERE conname = 'releases_project_id_version_key') THEN
    81→        ALTER TABLE releases ADD CONSTRAINT releases_project_id_version_key UNIQUE(project_id, version);
    82→    END IF;
    83→END $$;
    84→
    85→-- 4. Create agent_sessions table
    86→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS agent_sessions (
    87→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    88→    release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    89→    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    90→    status TEXT DEFAULT 'active',
    91→    tool_calls JSONB DEFAULT '[]',
    92→    findings JSONB DEFAULT '{}',
    93→    idle_deadline TIMESTAMPTZ,
    94→    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    95→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
    96→);
    97→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_agent_sessions_release ON agent_sessions(release_id);
    98→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_agent_sessions_project ON agent_sessions(project_id);
    99→
   100→-- 5. Drop pipeline_jobs table
   101→DROP TABLE IF EXISTS pipeline_jobs;
   102→
   103→-- 6. Update releases trigger to include project_id in notification
   104→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_release_created() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   105→BEGIN
   106→    PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', NEW.id::text);
   107→    RETURN NEW;
   108→END;
   109→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   110→
   111→-- 7. Remove poll_interval_seconds from sources (now on project)
   112→-- Keep column for now for backwards compat during migration, mark as deprecated
   113→-- We'll stop reading it in code
   114→```
   115→
   116→**Step 3: Run migration test**
   117→
   118→Run: `go build ./cmd/server && echo "migration compiles"`
   119→
   120→**Step 4: Commit**

   336→**Step 3: Write failing test for correlator**
   337→
   338→The correlator's job: given a new SourceRelease, find or create the matching project-scoped Release.
   339→
   340→```go
   341→// internal/correlation/correlator_test.go
   342→func TestCorrelator_Correlate(t *testing.T) {
   343→    // Test 1: new version creates new Release
   344→    // Test 2: same normalized version links to existing Release
   345→    // Test 3: different raw versions that normalize to same semver get linked
   346→}
   347→```
   348→
   349→**Step 4: Implement Correlator**
   350→
   351→```go
   352→// internal/correlation/correlator.go
   353→type CorrelationStore interface {
   354→    FindReleaseByVersion(ctx context.Context, projectID int, version string) (*models.Release, error)
   355→    CreateRelease(ctx context.Context, r *models.Release) error
   356→    LinkSourceRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceReleaseID string, releaseID string) error
   357→}
   358→
   359→type Correlator struct {
   360→    store CorrelationStore
   361→}
   362→
   363→func NewCorrelator(store CorrelationStore) *Correlator {
   364→    return &Correlator{store: store}
   365→}
   366→
   367→// Correlate takes a new SourceRelease and returns the correlated Release (found or created).
   368→func (c *Correlator) Correlate(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SourceRelease) (*models.Release, error) {
   369→    // 1. Parse version
   370→    sem, isPre, ok := ParseVersion(sr.RawVersion)
   371→
   372→    // 2. Build normalized version string
   373→    var version string
   374→    if ok {
   375→        version = sem.String()
   376→    } else {
   377→        version = sr.RawVersion // fallback to raw
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    // 3. Look for existing release with same project + version
   381→    existing, err := c.store.FindReleaseByVersion(ctx, sr.ProjectID, version)
   382→    if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {
   383→        return nil, err
   384→    }
   385→
   386→    if existing != nil {
   387→        // Link this source release to existing
   388→        c.store.LinkSourceRelease(ctx, sr.ID, existing.ID)
   389→        return existing, nil
   390→    }
   391→
   392→    // 4. Create new release
   393→    score, factors := ScoreUrgency(sem, isPre, sr.ReleaseNotes)
   394→    release := &models.Release{
   395→        ProjectID:    sr.ProjectID,
   396→        Version:      version,
   397→        Status:       "new",
   398→        UrgencyScore: &urgencyFloat, // convert score string to float
   399→        VersionInfo:  marshalVersionInfo(sem, isPre, factors),
   400→    }
   401→    err = c.store.CreateRelease(ctx, release)
   402→    if err != nil {
   403→        return nil, err
   404→    }
   405→
   406→    c.store.LinkSourceRelease(ctx, sr.ID, release.ID)
   407→    return release, nil
   408→}
   409→```
   410→
   411→Run: `go test ./internal/correlation/... -v` — expect PASS
   412→
   413→**Step 5: Commit**
   414→
   415→```bash
   416→git add internal/correlation/
   417→git commit -m "feat: add correlation engine for cross-source release linking"
   418→```
